_source line 289 · 2026-05-26T08:18:33.872Z_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：前向引用问题（先用后定义）

发现有些论文里出现"先用后定义"的情况——某个概念 / 符号 / 模块 / 缩写在 Section X 里直接用了，但它的定义放在 Section X+N 才出现。这是 AI 写作的典型问题，reviewer 读到这里会觉得"不知所云、组织混乱"。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
检查范围
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

每个东西第一次出现的位置，必须**已经**完成定义。包括：

□ 缩写 / 简写（如 RLHF / DPO / IRT，第一次出现要写全称 + 缩写）
□ 自定义概念 / 术语（你 paper 自己造的词，如 "behavioral death"、"sycophancy score"）
□ 数学符号 / 公式中的变量（每个变量在第一次出现的方程里就要解释）
□ 自定义方法 / 模块组件（"the consistency gate"、"the alignment head"）
□ 评测指标（自定义的 metric，必须在 main result 表出现之前定义清楚）
□ 数据集 / setting 的内部命名（"the strict split"、"the holdout pool"）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
常见违规模式
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

❌ Abstract / Intro 里用了一个自造术语，Method 才定义
❌ Intro 提到 "we use SYCONScore"，到 §4 才看到这个 metric 的定义
❌ Method §2 用了变量 α，到 §2.3 才写 "where α denotes..."
❌ Method 提到 "module M"，结构图 + 解释在 Appendix 才有
❌ Table 1 列了 "Score@K"，K 在 §5 才说取什么值

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
修复策略
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

每个违规位置二选一：

A) **把定义提前**到第一次使用之前。最干净。
B) 在第一次使用的位置**加一个简短的 inline 解释**（一句话 / 括号说明）+ "(formally defined in §X)" 引用前向。容忍度更高的妥协方案。

注意：A 优先。只有 A 会改动太多结构时，才退而求其次用 B。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 找到多少处先用后定义？分布在哪些章节？
(b) 每一处用 A 还是 B 修的？
(c) 修完后 PDF 编译通过

```text
Now I have a thorough picture of the paper. Let me compile the complete forward reference audit.

---

Here is the complete forward reference audit, organized by severity.

## CONFIRMED FORWARD REFERENCE ISSUES

### 1. **"DR" used without expansion in the abstract**
- **First use**: `abstract.tex`, line 3 -- "These domain/range violations (DR) persist even after fine-tuning..."
- **Status**: BORDERLINE OK. The full expansion "domain/range violations" appears on line 2 and the abbreviation "(DR)" is introduced parenthetically on line 3. This is acceptable as the expansion immediately precedes the abbreviation. However, on line 5 "DR" is used again without the expansion nearby -- this is fine since it was defined two sentences earlier. **No issue.**

### 2. **"CF" first used without expansion in the abstract**
- **First use**: `abstract.tex`, line 6 -- "converting irrecoverable type errors into correctable cross-field inconsistencies" -- CF is NOT used in the abstract, only the full phrase. OK.
- **First abbreviation use**: `section1_introduction_draft.tex` (Introduction), line 22 -- "cross-field type inconsistencies (CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\% fine-tuned)"
- **Definition**: `section3_violation_landscape.tex` (Experiments setup), line 17 -- "\textbf{CF} (conflicting types across triples)"
- **ISSUE**: CF is used in the **Introduction** (line 22) but only formally defined in the **Experiments** section. The introduction uses it parenthetically with a number, assuming the reader knows it is a metric abbreviation. The expansion "cross-field type inconsistencies" appears right before "(CF...)" so a reader can guess, but the formal meaning "conflicting types across triples" comes much later.
- **Severity**: MODERATE. The introduction provides enough context ("cross-field type inconsistencies") to be readable, but the precise definition as a violation category comes 4 sections later.

### 3. **"RV" and "ET" first used without expansion in the abstract/intro**
- **First use of "RV"**: `section5_crossmodel_draft.tex` (Cross-Model Validation), line 8 -- "S1 eliminates RV and ET for all models"
- **Definition of "RV"**: `section3_violation_landscape.tex`, line 17 -- "\textbf{RV} (relation $\notin \mathcal{R}$)"
- **Reading order**: The violation landscape setup (with definitions) appears BEFORE the cross-model subsection in reading order. **No issue** for main text flow.
- **BUT**: `main.tex` Limitations section, line 68 uses "RE" and "EE" without expansion (see below).

### 4. **"RE" used without ever being formally defined**
- **Uses in main text**: 
  - `section1_introduction_draft.tex`, line 20 -- "two RE datasets"
  - `section3_violation_landscape.tex`, line 9 -- "RE type constraints"
  - `main.tex` (Limitations), line 68 -- "two RE and one EE dataset"
- **Definition**: NEVER formally defined. The abstract says "relation extraction datasets" on line 6, but the abbreviation "RE" is never introduced with "(RE)".
- **ISSUE**: "RE" is used as a bare abbreviation starting in the Introduction without formal definition. While "RE" for "relation extraction" is common in the NLP community, the paper never writes "relation extraction (RE)" to establish it.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01DF49ZwGPgNK18CeoGEcVEf`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	We provide a detailed survey in Appendix~\ref{app:related_work}; here we position OntoCoDec along three axes.
5	
6	\paragraph{Constrained decoding.}
7	Grammar-constrained decoding restricts each token to a mask derived from a target grammar.
8	XGrammar~\cite{dong2025xgrammar}, Outlines~\cite{willard2023outlines}, SGLang~\cite{zheng2024sglang}, SynCode~\cite{ugare2024syncode}, and related systems~\cite{beurer-kellner2023lmql,park2024grammar,jsonschemabench2025} compile JSON Schemas into pushdown automata for structural enforcement, but are limited to context-free constraints and cannot enforce cross-field semantic rules that depend on previously generated field values.
9	PICARD~\cite{scholak2021picard} and \citet{pldi2025_type_constrained_codegen} compile type systems into prefix automata; KG-Trie~\cite{luo2025kgtrie} applies trie-based KG constraints for knowledge-to-text generation.
10	OntoCoDec fills this gap with a stateful semantic layer whose entity registry is populated dynamically during generation.
11	
12	\paragraph{Schema-aware IE.}
13	GoLLIE~\cite{sainz2024gollie}, KnowCoder~\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}, and others~\cite{guidex2025,josifoski2022genie,paolini2021tanl,cabot2021rebel,wang2023instructuie,qi2024adelie,fei2022lasuie,lu2022uie,zhou2024universalner} encode ontology structure via training-time internalization, learning schema preferences but without runtime guarantees: violations persist at 0.5--2\% even after fine-tuning~\cite{wadhwa2023revisiting}.
14	OntoCoDec provides orthogonal runtime enforcement composable with these systems, including GoLLIE itself (Appendix~\ref{app:gollie-comparison}).
15	
16	\paragraph{Constraint enforcement in IE.}
17	\citet{roth_yih_2004} and \citet{riedel2010modeling} formulated hard type constraints via ILP; post-hoc validation~\cite{khorshidi2025odke,spires_2024,ontologx_2025} detects violations after generation but discards up to 74\% of predictions; grammar-only enforcement~\cite{geng2023gcd,boostcd2025} achieves only 3\% DR reduction (\S\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).
18	EnriCo~\cite{zaratiana2024enrico} uses ASP; IterGen~\cite{ugare2025itergen} backtracks on violations; \citet{vukovic2024dialogue} combines ontology constraints with constrained chain-of-thought for dialogue RE.
19	These methods either operate post-hoc (discarding predictions) or require iterative refinement; OntoCoDec prevents violations during generation via forward-only stateful masking at $O(|V|)$ per step.
20	
```

_source line 298 · 2026-05-26T08:24:34.424Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01G61zAxZ9L3bAW9eiZ9CKDM`</summary>

```text
18	A decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\S\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the \emph{semantic layer} enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry.
19	OntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access.
20	We validate across three model families, two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
21	Composed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero ($\leq$\,0.21\%) at R-F1 40--46\% ($\pm$0.3\,pp, $+$10\% latency), with 97.6--100\% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\sim}$1.5$\times$ latency.
22	DR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF\,$\leq$\,1.4\% fine-tuned), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.
23	
24	OntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.
25	
26	Our contributions:
27	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
28	    \item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\leq$\,0.21\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \S\ref{sec:decidability}).
29	    \item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\% and semantic-only by 24\%, yet their composition achieves 94\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.
30	    \item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\% to 84\%; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
31	\end{enumerate}
32	
```

```text
1	% §5 Setup (compressed from Violation Landscape)
2	
3	\label{sec:violation_landscape}
4	
5	We evaluate OntoCoDec in two deployment configurations: greedy S1 (DR\,$\leq$\,0.21\%, $+$10\% latency) and beam S1 (near-zero violations, 1.39$\times$ latency); zero-shot evaluation (\S\ref{sec:scierc-results}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-zs}) serves as a diagnostic probe for the fine-tuned setting (relation F1 (R-F1) 40--46\%).
6	
7	\paragraph{Setup.}
8	We evaluate on SciERC~\cite{luan2018scierc} (551 test sentences, 7 relations, $\bar{\kappa}{=}0.333$) and DocRED~\cite{yao2019docred} (998 development documents; test annotations are not publicly available; 96 relations, $\bar{\kappa}{=}0.842$).
9	RE type constraints are extracted from observed type pairs in training data (neither dataset provides a standalone ontology specification); EE constraints on WikiEvents (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}) derive from the KAIROS ontology.
10	All constraints are schema-level, independent of evaluation splits.
11	Primary model: Qwen3-8B~\cite{qwen2025qwen3}, zero-shot greedy decoding; cross-model validation in Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-zs}, composability with fine-tuning in \S\ref{sec:composability}.
12	LoRA hyperparameters (rank, $\alpha$, learning rate) were tuned on SciERC and transferred to DocRED without dataset-specific optimization; given the substantial schema complexity difference (7 vs.\ 96 relations), this transfer may underestimate DocRED fine-tuning potential.
13	Our R-F1 range (40--46\% under LoRA) is consistent with recent LLM-based generative RE evaluations on these benchmarks, where document-level closed IE without gold entity spans typically achieves 35--50\%~\cite{wadhwa2023revisiting}; supervised span-based methods operating under different assumptions (gold entity spans, dedicated architectures) are not directly comparable.
14	For reference, the supervised end-to-end SOTA on SciERC achieves 41.6\% strict Rel-F1 (PL-Marker~\cite{ye2022packed}) and 36.8\% (PURE~\cite{zhong2021frustratingly}); while evaluation protocols differ, our 40--46\% R-F1 range under LoRA falls within a comparable band.
15	DocRED development documents were used for evaluation only.
16	Prompt templates and reproducibility details (hyperparameters, dataset statistics) in Appendices~\ref{app:prompts} and~\ref{app:reproducibility}.
17	Four violation categories: \textbf{DR} (type pair $\notin \sigma(r)$), \textbf{RV} (relation $\notin \mathcal{R}$), \textbf{ET} (entity type $\notin \mathcal{E}$), \textbf{CF} (conflicting types across triples).
18	Baseline DR: 15.37\% (SciERC), 18.46\% (DocRED); schema-informed prompting reduces DR by 62\% but is per-relation unreliable (Appendix~\ref{app:per-relation-landscape}).
19	
20	% Figure 6: Violation composition (placed before figure* to land on page 6)
```

```text
75	\emph{Does grammar-constrained decoding alone resolve DR violations?}
76	Table~\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\%, but their composition yields 94\%, the \emph{empirical enabling dependency}.
77	On DocRED (96 relations, $\bar{\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \emph{increases} DR via \textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).
78	Cross-model validation (3 models $\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\% on SciERC and 42--90\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\% but at 14--74\% rejection rates.
79	The DR-to-CF conversion is a severity downgrade, not an error introduction (Figure~\ref{fig:violation-composition}): DR violations produce ontology-invalid triples that corrupt downstream knowledge graphs and cannot be fixed without re-extraction, while CF (individually valid triples with cross-triple type inconsistency) can be resolved via fine-tuning, beam search, or post-hoc reranking (TCR eliminates all CF with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\ref{app:tcr}).
80	Zero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.
81	
82	% Figure 6 (violation-composition) moved earlier in source for page 6 placement
83	
84	% Cross-model table is in §6.1 (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model}).
```

```json
    "oldString": "We validate across three model families, two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).",
    "newString": "We validate across three model families, two relation extraction (RE) datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (EE; \\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).",
    "originalFile": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE)~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations} (DR): structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations are pervasive (15--18\\% of triples under zero-shot decoding and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning) and correlate with schema restrictiveness rather than model scale.\nAt competitive quality, even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering discards up to 74\\% of predictions including true positives (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the \\emph{semantic layer} enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access.\nWe validate across three model families, two RE datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nComposed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) at R-F1 40--46\\% ($\\pm$0.3\\,pp, $+$10\\% latency), with 97.6--100\\% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ latency.\nDR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% fine-tuned), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.\n\nOntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability}).\n    \\item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, yet their composition achieves 94\\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n    \\item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\\% to 84\\%; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

```json
    "originalFile": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE)~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations} (DR): structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations are pervasive (15--18\\% of triples under zero-shot decoding and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning) and correlate with schema restrictiveness rather than model scale.\nAt competitive quality, even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering discards up to 74\\% of predictions including true positives (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the \\emph{semantic layer} enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access.\nWe validate across three model families, two relation extraction (RE) datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (EE; \\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nComposed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) at relation F1 (R-F1) 40--46\\% ($\\pm$0.3 percentage points, $+$10\\% latency), with 97.6--100\\% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ latency.\nDR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% fine-tuned), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.\n\nOntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability}).\n    \\item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, yet their composition achieves 94\\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n    \\item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\\% to 84\\%; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

```json
{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "4baa9e8a-90e8-4104-8e87-0fa9bb7c8ca6",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "699511a1-46d9-4a49-84de-c6cafd200fcc",

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/section6_related_work.tex",
    "oldString": "formulated hard type constraints via ILP",
    "newString": "formulated hard type constraints via integer linear programming (ILP)",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\nWe provide a detailed survey in Appendix~\\ref{app:related_work}; here we position OntoCoDec along three axes.\n\n\\paragraph{Constrained decoding.}\nGrammar-constrained decoding restricts each token to a mask derived from a target grammar.\nXGrammar~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar}, Outlines~\\cite{willard2023outlines}, SGLang~\\cite{zheng2024sglang}, SynCode~\\cite{ugare2024syncode}, and related systems~\\cite{beurer-kellner2023lmql,park2024grammar,jsonschemabench2025} compile JSON Schemas into pushdown automata for structural enforcement, but are limited to context-free constraints and cannot enforce cross-field semantic rules that depend on previously generated field values.\nPICARD~\\cite{scholak2021picard} and \\citet{pldi2025_type_constrained_codegen} compile type systems into prefix automata; KG-Trie~\\cite{luo2025kgtrie} applies trie-based KG constraints for knowledge-to-text generation.\nOntoCoDec fills this gap with a stateful semantic layer whose entity registry is populated dynamically during generation.\n\n\\paragraph{Schema-aware IE.}\nGoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie}, KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}, and others~\\cite{guidex2025,josifoski2022genie,paolini2021tanl,cabot2021rebel,wang2023instructuie,qi2024adelie,fei2022lasuie,lu2022uie,zhou2024universalner} encode ontology structure via training-time internalization, learning schema preferences but without runtime guarantees: violations persist at 0.5--2\\% even after fine-tuning~\\cite{wadhwa2023revisiting}.\nOntoCoDec provides orthogonal runtime enforcement composable with these systems, including GoLLIE itself (Appendix~\\ref{app:gollie-comparison}).\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint enforcement in IE.}\n\\citet{roth_yih_2004} and \\citet{riedel2010modeling} formulated hard type constraints via ILP; post-hoc validation~\\cite{khorshidi2025odke,spires_2024,ontologx_2025} detects violations after generation but discards up to 74\\% of predictions; grammar-only enforcement~\\cite{geng2023gcd,boostcd2025} achieves only 3\\% DR reduction (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).\nEnriCo~\\cite{zaratiana2024enrico} uses ASP; IterGen~\\cite{ugare2025itergen} backtracks on violations; \\citet{vukovic2024dialogue} combines ontology constraints with constrained chain-of-thought for dialogue RE.\nThese methods either operate post-hoc (discarding predictions) or require iterative refinement; OntoCoDec prevents violations during generation via forward-only stateful masking at $O(|V|)$ per step.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 14,
        "oldLines": 6,
        "newStart": 14,
        "newLines": 6,

          "+\\citet{roth_yih_2004} and \\citet{riedel2010modeling} formulated hard type constraints via integer linear programming (ILP); post-hoc validation~\\cite{khorshidi2025odke,spires_2024,ontologx_2025} detects violations after generation but discards up to 74\\% of predictions; grammar-only enforcement~\\cite{geng2023gcd,boostcd2025} achieves only 3\\% DR reduction (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).",
```

```json
  "content": "[飞书用户 孙震 (ou_bd765366c8b4c0e5f6af6257391ecb88)]: 优点\n\n  1. 问题定义极其精准，一句话就抓住了\n\n  ▎ \"structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's \n  ▎ domain/range constraints\"\n\n  开篇第二句就用一个精确的技术定义把问题框死了。Reviewer\n  不需要猜\"这篇到底在解决什么\"。这比上一篇需要读两段才明白问题要好得多。\n\n  2. 现有方法的不足分析非常有层次\n\n  Intro 第二段用三句话精准拆解了三类现有方法各自的局限：\n  - Schema-informed prompting：per-relation 不可靠，某些关系反而 increase violations\n  - Grammar-constrained decoding：只能保证结构合法，不能跨字段语义约束\n  - Post-hoc filtering：丢弃高达 74% 的预测\n\n  每个都有具体数字支撑，不是空泛的 \"existing methods have limitations\"。这让 reviewer\n  觉得作者真正理解了 landscape。\n\n  3. \"两层非加性交互\"是一个有记忆点的发现\n\n  \"grammar-only reduces DR by 3% and semantic-only by 24%, yet their composition achieves 94%\" —\n  这个 3.5× 的超加性效应非常 striking，作为 contribution 2 很有说服力。\n\n  4. 实际影响的叙述很务实\n\n  \"even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic\n  knowledge graph corruption\" — 这句把一个看似小问题（0.5-2% violation\n  rate）与大规模应用后果联系起来，justification 很实际。\n\n  ---\n  问题与不足\n\n  1. Abstract 同样有信息过载问题，但方向不同\n\n  上一篇是数字太多，这篇是概念/术语太密集。一个 abstract\n  里出现了：DR、CF、EDCR（不对，这篇没有）、grammar layer、semantic layer、entity\n  registry、stateful masking、token-level masks、context-free grammars、prefix-decidable、beam\n  search、cross-field type inconsistencies……reviewer 第一遍扫读会觉得信息密度过高。\n\n  建议：Abstract 应该聚焦在 what（问题）→ why hard（为什么现有方法不行）→ \n  how（我们的方案核心思路）→ result（效果），技术细节（stateful masking、entity registry\n  等）留给正文。\n\n  2. Introduction 缺少一个 \"aha moment\"\n\n  上一篇有 \"our experiments show this expectation is wrong\"\n  这种转折，这篇没有。它的叙事是线性的：问题 → 现有方法不行 → 我们的方案 →\n  贡献。逻辑上完全正确，但没有 surprise。\n\n  一个可能的转折点其实就藏在文中但没有被突出：\n\n  ▎ \"The two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3% and semantic-only by \n  ▎ 24%, yet their composition achieves 94%.\"\n\n  这个发现如果在 intro 开头就作为一个 puzzle 抛出（\"Why does combining two weak constraint layers\n  yield 94% violation reduction?\"），然后用后面的 decidability analysis 来解释，叙事会有趣得多。\n\n  3. Contribution 3（70B scale validation）作为 contribution 偏弱\n\n  \"Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B\" — 这更像是一个 ablation 或 robustness\n   check，不像一个独立的 contribution。如果只有三个 contributions，前两个很强，第三个最好也是一个\n  insight 而非 \"we also tested on a bigger model\"。\n\n  4. 标题很好，但 \"Compiled Schema Constraints\" 可能让人误以为是 PL/编译器论文\n\n  \"OntoCoDec: Type-Constrained Information Extraction via Compiled Schema Constraints\" — 对 NLP\n  reviewer 来说，\"compiled\" 和 \"type-constrained\"\n  可能第一反应是编程语言方向的工作。不过这个问题不大，abstract 第一句就纠正了。\n\n  5. Intro 的 \"we propose\" 出现得偏晚\n\n  Intro 大概在第三段才出现 \"We propose\n  OntoCoDec\"，前面花了大量篇幅分析现有方法。对于方法论文来说，reviewer 希望更早知道\"你要做什么\"。\n\n看下对你的abstract和introduction的审核意见，你分析一下"
}
```

  1. 问题定义极其精准，一句话就抓住了

  ▎ "structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's 
  ▎ domain/range constraints"

  开篇第二句就用一个精确的技术定义把问题框死了。Reviewer
  不需要猜"这篇到底在解决什么"。这比上一篇需要读两段才明白问题要好得多。

  2. 现有方法的不足分析非常有层次

  Intro 第二段用三句话精准拆解了三类现有方法各自的局限：
  - Schema-informed prompting：per-relation 不可靠，某些关系反而 increase violations
  - Grammar-constrained decoding：只能保证结构合法，不能跨字段语义约束
  - Post-hoc filtering：丢弃高达 74% 的预测

  每个都有具体数字支撑，不是空泛的 "existing methods have limitations"。这让 reviewer
  觉得作者真正理解了 landscape。

  3. "两层非加性交互"是一个有记忆点的发现

  "grammar-only reduces DR by 3% and semantic-only by 24%, yet their composition achieves 94%" —
  这个 3.5× 的超加性效应非常 striking，作为 contribution 2 很有说服力。

  4. 实际影响的叙述很务实

  "even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic
  knowledge graph corruption" — 这句把一个看似小问题（0.5-2% violation
  rate）与大规模应用后果联系起来，justification 很实际。

  ---
  问题与不足

  1. Abstract 同样有信息过载问题，但方向不同

  上一篇是数字太多，这篇是概念/术语太密集。一个 abstract
  里出现了：DR、CF、EDCR（不对，这篇没有）、grammar layer、semantic layer、entity
  registry、stateful masking、token-level masks、context-free grammars、prefix-decidable、beam
  search、cross-field type inconsistencies……reviewer 第一遍扫读会觉得信息密度过高。

  建议：Abstract 应该聚焦在 what（问题）→ why hard（为什么现有方法不行）→ 
  how（我们的方案核心思路）→ result（效果），技术细节（stateful masking、entity registry
  等）留给正文。

  2. Introduction 缺少一个 "aha moment"

  上一篇有 "our experiments show this expectation is wrong"
  这种转折，这篇没有。它的叙事是线性的：问题 → 现有方法不行 → 我们的方案 →
  贡献。逻辑上完全正确，但没有 surprise。

  一个可能的转折点其实就藏在文中但没有被突出：

  ▎ "The two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3% and semantic-only by 
  ▎ 24%, yet their composition achieves 94%."

  这个发现如果在 intro 开头就作为一个 puzzle 抛出（"Why does combining two weak constraint layers
  yield 94% violation reduction?"），然后用后面的 decidability analysis 来解释，叙事会有趣得多。

  3. Contribution 3（70B scale validation）作为 contribution 偏弱

  "Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B" — 这更像是一个 ablation 或 robustness
   check，不像一个独立的 contribution。如果只有三个 contributions，前两个很强，第三个最好也是一个
  insight 而非 "we also tested on a bigger model"。

  4. 标题很好，但 "Compiled Schema Constraints" 可能让人误以为是 PL/编译器论文

  "OntoCoDec: Type-Constrained Information Extraction via Compiled Schema Constraints" — 对 NLP
  reviewer 来说，"compiled" 和 "type-constrained"
  可能第一反应是编程语言方向的工作。不过这个问题不大，abstract 第一句就纠正了。

  5. Intro 的 "we propose" 出现得偏晚

  Intro 大概在第三段才出现 "We propose
  OntoCoDec"，前面花了大量篇幅分析现有方法。对于方法论文来说，reviewer 希望更早知道"你要做什么"。

看下对你的abstract和introduction的审核意见，你分析一下

```json
{
  "text": "孙震老师的意见分析：\n\n**优点部分**——确认了我们的核心叙事策略有效：问题定义精准、gap 分析有层次、非加性交互有记忆点、实际影响 justification 务实。这些都不需要改。\n\n**问题部分逐条判断：**\n\n**1. Abstract 信息过载** — 同意。当前 abstract 确实术语密集（grammar layer / semantic layer / entity registry / stateful masking / token-level masks 全塞在一起）。但论文已封版，如果要改 abstract 需要你确认。改法：去掉实现细节词（\"stateful masking\"、\"token-level masks\"），只保留 what/why/how/result 骨架，约砍 2-3 个从句。\n\n**2. Intro 缺 aha moment** — 分析很准。非加性交互（3%+24%→94%）确实是最 striking 的发现，目前埋在第三段。如果把它提到 Intro 开头作为 puzzle（\"Why does combining two individually weak constraint layers yield 94% reduction?\"），再用 decidability analysis 作为 payoff，叙事张力更强。但这是结构性重写 Intro，封版状态下改动较大。\n\n**3. Contribution 3 偏弱** — 同意。\"Supporting validation\" 的措辞本身就暗示这不是一个独立 insight。但 70B 的 semantic-only DR reduction 从 24%→84% 其实有独立的 scaling insight（\"semantic layer gains more from scale than grammar layer\"）。如果改措辞强调 scaling 发现本身而非\"我们也测了大模型\"，可以升级。但同样涉及改论文。\n\n**4. 标题 \"Compiled\" 误解** — 孙震老师自己也说\"问题不大\"，不改。\n\n**5. \"We propose\" 出现偏晚** — 有道理但属于 Intro 结构偏好问题。当前结构（problem→gap→solution）是标准的，只是 gap 分析篇幅略长。\n\n**总结**：5 条意见中 1/2/3 有道理且可操作，但都涉及改 abstract/intro 正文。论文已封版——是否要针对这些意见做最后一轮修改？如果要改，我建议只动 abstract（砍术语密度）和 contribution 3 措辞（1 句话），不动 Intro 结构（改动太大风险高）。"
}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Mx3Y9BxgPQdvZSBempzNF4`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Large language models for information extraction generate structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's domain/range constraints for the predicted relation.
3	These domain/range violations (DR) persist even after fine-tuning and cannot be resolved by grammar-constrained decoding, which lacks cross-field type awareness.
4	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec} (\textbf{Onto}logy-\textbf{Co}mpiled \textbf{Dec}oding), a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into token-level masks: a grammar layer enforces structural validity; a semantic layer enforces type constraints at runtime via stateful masking over a dynamic entity registry.
5	The two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\% and semantic-only by 24\%, yet their composition achieves 94\%.
6	Composed with LoRA fine-tuning across three model families and two relation extraction datasets (up to 96 relations), OntoCoDec reduces DR to $\leq$\,0.21\% with minimal F1 impact ($\pm$0.3\,pp), converting irrecoverable type errors into correctable cross-field inconsistencies; beam search ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\sim}$1.5$\times$ unconstrained latency.
7	Validation on a 70B model shows the semantic layer's independent effectiveness grows from 24\% to 84\% DR reduction with scale\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/schema_semantic_constrained_ie-FE20/}.}.
8	\end{abstract}
9	
```

```json
      "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nLarge language models for information extraction generate structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's domain/range constraints for the predicted relation.\nThese domain/range violations (DR) persist even after fine-tuning and cannot be resolved by grammar-constrained decoding, which lacks cross-field type awareness.\nWe present \\textsc{OntoCoDec} (\\textbf{Onto}logy-\\textbf{Co}mpiled \\textbf{Dec}oding), a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into token-level masks: a grammar layer enforces structural validity; a semantic layer enforces type constraints at runtime via stateful masking over a dynamic entity registry.\nThe two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, yet their composition achieves 94\\%.\nComposed with LoRA fine-tuning across three model families and two relation extraction datasets (up to 96 relations), OntoCoDec reduces DR to $\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with minimal F1 impact ($\\pm$0.3\\,pp), converting irrecoverable type errors into correctable cross-field inconsistencies; beam search ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ unconstrained latency.\nValidation on a 70B model shows the semantic layer's independent effectiveness grows from 24\\% to 84\\% DR reduction with scale\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/schema_semantic_constrained_ie-FE20/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
```

```text
26	Our contributions:
27	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
28	    \item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\leq$\,0.21\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \S\ref{sec:decidability}).
29	    \item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\% and semantic-only by 24\%, yet their composition achieves 94\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.
30	    \item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\% to 84\%; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
31	\end{enumerate}
32
```

_source line 359 · 2026-05-26T08:35:44.428Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now let me plan the changes:

**1. Abstract — 砍术语密度**

Current line 4 has: "a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into token-level masks: a grammar layer enforces structural validity; a semantic layer enforces type constraints at runtime via stateful masking over a dynamic entity registry."

The problem: "token-level masks", "stateful masking", "dynamic entity registry" are implementation details that can go to the Method section. The abstract should convey the core idea without these.

Revised line 4: "a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into composable decoding constraints: a grammar layer enforces structural validity via context-free grammars; a semantic layer enforces cross-field type constraints at runtime."


This removes the implementation-specific terminology while preserving the two-layer architecture and what each layer accomplishes. I want to keep "decoding-time constraints" and "dynamically enforces" to show this happens during generation, but strip out the technical details about masking mechanisms and entity registries.

Now for the third contribution, I need to distill the validation results into something more concise. The current version lists specific metrics and section references, but I can compress this to focus on the key findings: the approach scales well with model size, achieves strong constraint satisfaction, and generalizes across datasets. The key insight is that larger models better internalize the vocabulary constraints needed for semantic enforcement to work—the independent effectiveness jumps dramatically from 24% to 84% as we scale from 8B to 70B parameters. Beyond that scaling finding, beam search with k=4 nearly eliminates violations at 8B, and the approach generalizes across tasks like event extraction on WikiEvents.

I'm also deciding what to keep stable: the Introduction structure works fine as-is with problem, gap, and solution laid out across three paragraphs before the "we propose" statement—that's standard for method papers and the non-additive interaction finding is already highlighted as a contribution. Restructuring at submission time would risk more than it gains.

Now I need to verify the abstract word count after making these changes.
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex",
        "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nLarge language models for information extraction generate structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's domain/range constraints for the predicted relation.\nThese domain/range violations (DR) persist even after fine-tuning and cannot be resolved by grammar-constrained decoding, which lacks cross-field type awareness.\nWe present \\textsc{OntoCoDec} (\\textbf{Onto}logy-\\textbf{Co}mpiled \\textbf{Dec}oding), a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into decoding-time constraints: a grammar layer enforces structural validity; a semantic layer dynamically enforces cross-field type constraints during generation.\nThe two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, yet their composition achieves 94\\%.\nComposed with LoRA fine-tuning across three model families and two relation extraction datasets (up to 96 relations), OntoCoDec reduces DR to $\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with minimal F1 impact ($\\pm$0.3\\,pp), converting irrecoverable type errors into correctable cross-field inconsistencies; beam search ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ unconstrained latency.\nValidation on a 70B model shows the semantic layer's independent effectiveness grows from 24\\% to 84\\% DR reduction with scale\\footnote{Code and data are available at \\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/schema_semantic_constrained_ie-FE20/}.}.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
        "numLines": 9,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 9
      }
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/section6_related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\nWe provide a detailed survey in Appendix~\\ref{app:related_work}; here we position OntoCoDec along three axes.\n\n\\paragraph{Constrained decoding.}\nGrammar-constrained decoding restricts each token to a mask derived from a target grammar.\nXGrammar~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar}, Outlines~\\cite{willard2023outlines}, SGLang~\\cite{zheng2024sglang}, SynCode~\\cite{ugare2024syncode}, and related systems~\\cite{beurer-kellner2023lmql,park2024grammar,jsonschemabench2025} compile JSON Schemas into pushdown automata for structural enforcement, but are limited to context-free constraints and cannot enforce cross-field semantic rules that depend on previously generated field values.\nPICARD~\\cite{scholak2021picard} and \\citet{pldi2025_type_constrained_codegen} compile type systems into prefix automata; KG-Trie~\\cite{luo2025kgtrie} applies trie-based KG constraints for knowledge-to-text generation.\nOntoCoDec fills this gap with a stateful semantic layer whose entity registry is populated dynamically during generation.\n\n\\paragraph{Schema-aware IE.}\nGoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie}, KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}, and others~\\cite{guidex2025,josifoski2022genie,paolini2021tanl,cabot2021rebel,wang2023instructuie,qi2024adelie,fei2022lasuie,lu2022uie,zhou2024universalner} encode ontology structure via training-time internalization, learning schema preferences but without runtime guarantees: violations persist at 0.5--2\\% even after fine-tuning~\\cite{wadhwa2023revisiting}.\nOntoCoDec provides orthogonal runtime enforcement composable with these systems, including GoLLIE itself (Appendix~\\ref{app:gollie-comparison}).\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint enforcement in IE.}\n\\citet{roth_yih_2004} and \\citet{riedel2010modeling} formulated hard type constraints via integer linear programming (ILP); post-hoc validation~\\cite{khorshidi2025odke,spires_2024,ontologx_2025} detects violations after generation but discards up to 74\\% of predictions; grammar-only enforcement~\\cite{geng2023gcd,boostcd2025} achieves only 3\\% DR reduction (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).\nEnriCo~\\cite{zaratiana2024enrico} uses answer set programming (ASP); IterGen~\\cite{ugare2025itergen} backtracks on violations; \\citet{vukovic2024dialogue} combines ontology constraints with constrained chain-of-thought for dialogue RE.\nThese methods either operate post-hoc (discarding predictions) or require iterative refinement; OntoCoDec prevents violations during generation via forward-only stateful masking at $O(|V|)$ per step.\n",
        "numLines": 20,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 20
      }
    },

only the active constraint layers differ across rows.\n\n% Figure 5 (scale-dependency) moved to figure* in section3_violation_landscape.tex\n\n% Figure 6 (violation-composition) moved to section3_violation_landscape.tex for page 6 placement\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  Table: SciERC five-way ablation\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3.6pt}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}l rrrrrr rr@{}}\n\\toprule\n& \\multicolumn{6}{c}{\\textbf{Violation Rates (\\%)}}\n& \\multicolumn{2}{c}{\\textbf{F1}} \\\\\n\\cmidrule(lr){2-7} \\cmidrule(l){8-9}\n\\textbf{Mode}\n  & DR\\,$\\downarrow$ & $\\Delta$DR\n  & RV\\,$\\downarrow$ & ET\\,$\\downarrow$ & CF & Tot.\\,$\\downarrow$\n  & Rel.\\,$\\uparrow$ & Ent.\\,$\\uparrow$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nUnconstrained\n  & 15.37 & ---\n  & 1.39  & 0.40\n  & 0.08  & 15.45\n  & 8.23  & 31.01 \\\\\nGrammar\n  & 14.84 & $\\downarrow$3\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.08  & 14.92\n  & 8.23  & 30.98 \\\\\nGCD-equiv.$^\\dagger$\n  & 15.26 & $\\downarrow$1\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 0.04  & 15.30\n  & 8.75  & 30.91 \\\\\nSemantic\n  & 11.65 & $\\downarrow$24\\%\n  & 1.51  & 0.40\n  & 2.71  & 14.36\n  & 8.15  & 31.01 \\\\\nS1\n  & \\textbf{0.93} & $\\downarrow$\\textbf{94\\%}\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 9.90  & \\textbf{10.83}\n  & \\textbf{8.36} & 30.98 \\\\\nS2\n  & 0.97  & $\\downarrow$94\\%\n  & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0}\n  & 8.03  & \\textbf{9.00}\n  & 8.03  & \\textbf{31.90} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}%\n\\caption{Five-way ablation on SciERC (551 docs, Qwen3-8B, zero-shot).\n$\\Delta$DR: relative change vs.\\ unconstrained; Tot.\\ = DR + CF; bold = best (CF excluded).\nF1 (\\%). S1/S2 = single/two-pass (\\S\\ref{sec:strategies}).\nGrammar = grammar-constrained decoding (GCD) ceiling~\\cite{geng2023gcd}.\n$\\dagger$vLLM \\texttt{guided\\_json}~\\cite{kwon2023vllm} (\\S\\ref{sec:constrained-baselines}).\nDeterministic (greedy, $T{=}$0); robustness evidence in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model} and Appendices~\\ref{app:bootstrap-effect-sizes},~\\ref{app:prompt-sensitivity},~\\ref{app:doc-length},~\\ref{app:sampling}.}\n\\label{tab:scierc-ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (a) Zero-shot diagnostic (compressed)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Zero-shot diagnostic.}\n\\emph{Does grammar-constrained decoding alone resolve DR violations?}\nTable~\\ref{tab:scierc-ablation} establishes that DR violations are pervasive (15--18\\% across datasets) and that constraint layers interact non-additively: grammar-only achieves 3\\% DR reduction on SciERC, semantic-only 24\\%, but their composition yields 94\\%, the \\emph{empirical enabling dependency}.\nOn DocRED (96 relations, $\\bar{\\kappa}{=}0.842$), grammar-only \\emph{increases} DR via \\textit{latent violation surfacing}: forcing out-of-vocabulary (OOV)$\\to$valid relation mapping exposes violations previously masked by vocabulary errors (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:docred-zs-ablation}).\nCross-model validation (3 models $\\times$ 2 datasets; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}) confirms DR drops of 76--99\\% on SciERC and 42--90\\% on DocRED, with R-F1 improving in 5/6 settings; post-hoc filtering achieves DR${=}$0\\% but at 14--74\\% rejection rates.\nThe DR-to-CF conversion is a severity downgrade, not an error introduction (Figure~\\ref{fig:violation-composition}): DR violations produce ontology-invalid triples that corrupt downstream knowledge graphs and cannot be fixed without re-extraction, while CF (individually valid triples with cross-triple type inconsistency) can be resolved via fine-tuning, beam search, or post-hoc type-consistency reranking (TCR; eliminates all CF with bounded DR regression; Appendix~\\ref{app:tcr}).\nZero-shot evaluation thus establishes violation prevalence and constraint interaction; fine-tuning demonstrates practical elimination.\n\n% Figure 6 (violation-composition) moved earlier in source for page 6 placement\n\n% Cross-model table is in §6.1 (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model}).\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (e) Composability with Fine-Tuning (expanded)\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Composability with fine-tuning.}\n\\label{sec:composability}\n\\emph{Does OntoCoDec retain value after fine-tuning?}\nFine-tuning alone reduces DR to 0.5--2\\% but leaves residual violations on tight-constraint relations (Table~\\ref{tab:composability}; full fine-tuning in Appendix~\\ref{app:full-ft}); adding S1 yields DR\\,$\\leq$\\,0.21\\% with no significant $\\Delta$R-F1 (Bonferroni-corrected; 95\\% CIs $\\subseteq [-0.69, {+}0.25]$\\,pp; removed triples are 97.6--100\\% false positives; Appendix~\\ref{app:downstream-impact}).\nUnder fine-tuning, grammar becomes a no-op ($\\Delta$DR$<$0.2\\,pp), making the semantic layer the sole DR defense (41--69\\% on SciERC, 2--75\\% on DocRED; Appendix~\\ref{app:ft-ablation}); three relations with $\\kappa \\geq 0.36$ retain DR, all eliminated by S1 (Appendix~\\ref{app:lora-per-relation}).\nCF drops from $+$9.82\\,pp (zero-shot) to $+$1.18\\,pp (fine-tuned), concentrated in \\textsc{Generic}$\\leftrightarrow$X ambiguity (63.3\\%; Appendices~\\ref{app:cf-distribution},~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}); beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at 8B (R-F1 43--46; \\S\\ref{sec:beam-results}).\n\n% CF analysis folded into composability paragraph above; details in Appendices~\\ref{app:cf-distribution},~\\ref{app:cascade-analysis}.\n% Table 2 (composability) moved to section5_crossmodel_draft.tex for page 8 placement\n\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n%  (f) Downstream impact of DR elimination\n% ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\\paragraph{Downstream impact.}\n\\label{sec:downstream-impact}\nConstraint removal affects only 0.24\\% of gold triples; error analysis confirms only 0.2\\% of false negatives were blocked by constraints (Appendices~\\ref{app:lucky-violations},~\\ref{app:error-analysis},~\\ref{app:case-studies}).\nThe 36 residual DR violations across fine-tuned settings decompose into entity type errors (39\\%), schema gaps (33\\%), and relation/direction errors (28\\%); the irreducible floor is $\\sim$3 violations (Appendix~\\ref{app:residual-dr-analysis}).\n",

      }
```

```json
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/section1_introduction_draft.tex",
        "content": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE)~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations} (DR): structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations are pervasive (15--18\\% of triples under zero-shot decoding and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning) and correlate with schema restrictiveness rather than model scale.\nAt competitive quality, even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering discards up to 74\\% of predictions including true positives (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the \\emph{semantic layer} enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access.\nWe validate across three model families, two relation extraction (RE) datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (EE; \\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nComposed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) at relation F1 (R-F1) 40--46\\% ($\\pm$0.3 percentage points, $+$10\\% latency), with 97.6--100\\% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ latency.\nDR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF;\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% under fine-tuning), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.\n\nOntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability}).\n    \\item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, yet their composition achieves 94\\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n    \\item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\\% to 84\\%; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 32,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 32
      }
    },
```

```text
26	Our contributions:
27	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
28	    \item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\leq$\,0.21\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \S\ref{sec:decidability}).
29	    \item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\% and semantic-only by 24\%, yet their composition achieves 94\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.
30	    \item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\% to 84\%; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
31	\end{enumerate}
```

```json
    "newString": "    \\item Scale-dependent layer interaction: the semantic layer's independent DR reduction grows from 24\\% (8B) to 84\\% (70B; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}), because larger models better satisfy the vocabulary prerequisites that enable semantic enforcement. Beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction on WikiEvents confirms cross-task generalization (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).",
    "originalFile": "% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nLarge language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE)~\\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.\nWe call such outputs \\emph{domain/range violations} (DR): structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.\nViolations are pervasive (15--18\\% of triples under zero-shot decoding and 0.5--2\\% even after LoRA fine-tuning) and correlate with schema restrictiveness rather than model scale.\nAt competitive quality, even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap.\nSchema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \\textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \\emph{increase} by 5.8\\,pp).\nGrammar-constrained decoding~\\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.\nPost-hoc filtering discards up to 74\\% of predictions including true positives (Appendices~\\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).\nTraining-based approaches~\\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.\n\nWe propose \\textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.\nA decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \\emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the \\emph{semantic layer} enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry.\nOntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access.\nWe validate across three model families, two relation extraction (RE) datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (EE; \\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}).\nComposed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) at relation F1 (R-F1) 40--46\\% ($\\pm$0.3 percentage points, $+$10\\% latency), with 97.6--100\\% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\\sim}$1.5$\\times$ latency.\nDR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF;\\,$\\leq$\\,1.4\\% under fine-tuning), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.\n\nOntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]\n    \\item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\\leq$\\,0.21\\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \\S\\ref{sec:decidability}).\n    \\item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\\% and semantic-only by 24\\%, yet their composition achieves 94\\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n    \\item Supporting validation: scale-dependent interaction at 70B (semantic-only DR reduction grows from 24\\% to 84\\%; \\S\\ref{sec:70b-results}); beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction generalization on WikiEvents (\\S\\ref{sec:wikievents}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01RawRck1zFR782LvtXT1svZ`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	Large language models for information extraction generate structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's domain/range constraints for the predicted relation.
3	These domain/range violations (DR) persist even after fine-tuning and cannot be resolved by grammar-constrained decoding, which lacks cross-field type awareness.
4	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec} (\textbf{Onto}logy-\textbf{Co}mpiled \textbf{Dec}oding), a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into decoding-time constraints: a grammar layer enforces structural validity; a semantic layer dynamically enforces cross-field type constraints during generation.
5	The two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\% and semantic-only by 24\%, yet their composition achieves 94\%.
6	Composed with LoRA fine-tuning across three model families and two relation extraction datasets (up to 96 relations), OntoCoDec reduces DR to $\leq$\,0.21\% with minimal F1 impact ($\pm$0.3\,pp), converting irrecoverable type errors into correctable cross-field inconsistencies; beam search ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\sim}$1.5$\times$ unconstrained latency.
7	Validation on a 70B model shows the semantic layer's independent effectiveness grows from 24\% to 84\% DR reduction with scale\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/schema_semantic_constrained_ie-FE20/}.}.
8	\end{abstract}
9
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016S5o3irH8zLUqNQisJFeqT`</summary>

```text
1	% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	\label{sec:introduction}
5	
6	Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE)~\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.
7	We call such outputs \emph{domain/range violations} (DR): structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.
8	Violations are pervasive (15--18\% of triples under zero-shot decoding and 0.5--2\% even after LoRA fine-tuning) and correlate with schema restrictiveness rather than model scale.
9	At competitive quality, even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.
10	
11	Existing approaches leave a fundamental gap.
12	Schema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \emph{increase} by 5.8\,pp).
13	Grammar-constrained decoding~\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.
14	Post-hoc filtering discards up to 74\% of predictions including true positives (Appendices~\ref{app:cross-model-zs},~\ref{app:posthoc-extended}).
15	Training-based approaches~\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.
16	
17	We propose \textbf{OntoCoDec}, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints.
18	A decidability analysis of IE type constraints (\S\ref{sec:decidability}) yields a two-layer architecture: the \emph{grammar layer} compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the \emph{semantic layer} enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry.
19	OntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access.
20	We validate across three model families, two relation extraction (RE) datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (EE; \S\ref{sec:wikievents}), with 70B-class supporting evidence (\S\ref{sec:70b-results}).
21	Composed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero ($\leq$\,0.21\%) at relation F1 (R-F1) 40--46\% ($\pm$0.3 percentage points, $+$10\% latency), with 97.6--100\% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam ($k{=}4$) achieves near-zero violations at ${\sim}$1.5$\times$ latency.
22	DR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF;\,$\leq$\,1.4\% under fine-tuning), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.
23	
24	OntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE~\cite{sainz2024gollie} and KnowCoder~\cite{knowcoder_acl2024}; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.
25	
26	Our contributions:
27	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
28	    \item A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero ($\leq$\,0.21\%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition~1; \S\ref{sec:decidability}).
29	    \item An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3\% and semantic-only by 24\%, yet their composition achieves 94\%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.
30	    \item Scale-dependent layer interaction: the semantic layer's independent DR reduction grows from 24\% (8B) to 84\% (70B; \S\ref{sec:70b-results}), because larger models better satisfy the vocabulary prerequisites that enable semantic enforcement. Beam $k{=}4$ achieves near-zero violations at 8B; event extraction on WikiEvents confirms cross-task generalization (\S\ref{sec:wikievents}).
31	\end{enumerate}
32	
```

```json
{
  "text": "@孙震 修改后的 Abstract 和 Introduction：\n\n---\n\n**Abstract:**\n\nLarge language models for information extraction generate structurally valid but ontologically invalid triples: entity types that violate the schema's domain/range constraints for the predicted relation. These domain/range violations (DR) persist even after fine-tuning and cannot be resolved by grammar-constrained decoding, which lacks cross-field type awareness. We present OntoCoDec (Ontology-Compiled Decoding), a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontologies into decoding-time constraints: a grammar layer enforces structural validity; a semantic layer dynamically enforces cross-field type constraints during generation. The two layers interact non-additively: grammar-only reduces DR by 3% and semantic-only by 24%, yet their composition achieves 94%. Composed with LoRA fine-tuning across three model families and two relation extraction datasets (up to 96 relations), OntoCoDec reduces DR to ≤0.21% with minimal F1 impact (±0.3 pp), converting irrecoverable type errors into correctable cross-field inconsistencies; beam search (k=4) achieves near-zero violations at ~1.5× unconstrained latency. Validation on a 70B model shows the semantic layer's independent effectiveness grows from 24% to 84% DR reduction with scale.\n\n---\n\n**Section 1: Introduction**\n\nLarge language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for information extraction (IE), but when generating relation triples (h, r, t), neither entity necessarily carries a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for r. We call such outputs *domain/range violations* (DR): structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid. Violations are pervasive (15–18% of triples under zero-shot decoding and 0.5–2% even after LoRA fine-tuning) and correlate with schema restrictiveness rather than model scale. At competitive quality, even low residual violation rates compound across thousands of documents into systematic knowledge graph corruption, making reliable constraint enforcement necessary.\n\nExisting approaches leave a fundamental gap. Schema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62% on SciERC but is per-relation unreliable (violations for Evaluate-for actually *increase* by 5.8 pp). Grammar-constrained decoding ensures syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints. Post-hoc filtering discards up to 74% of predictions including true positives. Training-based approaches internalize schema knowledge but provide only soft guidance without runtime enforcement.\n\nWe propose **OntoCoDec**, a framework that translates IE ontologies into composable layers of decoding-time constraints. A decidability analysis of IE type constraints (§2) yields a two-layer architecture: the *grammar layer* compiles ontology constraints into context-free grammars (CFGs) via XGrammar (JSON structure, vocabulary membership); the *semantic layer* enforces type constraints at runtime via dynamic logit masking over an entity registry. OntoCoDec compiles constraints automatically from IE ontologies, requires no constraint-specific training, and is applicable to any autoregressive LM with logits access. We validate across three model families, two relation extraction (RE) datasets (up to 96 relation types), and event extraction (EE; §5.2), with 70B-class supporting evidence (§5.3). Composed with LoRA, greedy decoding reduces DR to near-zero (≤0.21%) at relation F1 (R-F1) 40–46% (±0.3 percentage points, +10% latency), with 97.6–100% of removed triples confirmed as false positives; beam (k=4) achieves near-zero violations at ~1.5× latency. DR enforcement converts irrecoverable ontology violations into cross-field type inconsistencies (CF; ≤1.4% under fine-tuning), a severity downgrade amenable to post-hoc resolution.\n\nOntoCoDec is orthogonal and complementary to training-based schema-aware approaches such as GoLLIE and KnowCoder; it can be composed with any generative IE system to improve schema compliance without retraining.\n\nOur contributions:\n1. A two-layer constrained decoding framework that reduces domain/range violations to near-zero (≤0.21%) without retraining or significant quality loss, showing that these type constraints require stateful enforcement beyond context-free grammars (Proposition 1; §2).\n2. An empirical finding of non-additive layer interaction: grammar-only reduces DR by 3% and semantic-only by 24%, yet their composition achieves 94%, because grammar-enforced vocabulary membership creates the clean input the semantic layer requires.\n3. Scale-dependent layer interaction: the semantic layer's independent DR reduction grows from 24% (8B) to 84% (70B; §5.3), because larger models better satisfy the vocabulary prerequisites that enable semantic enforcement.   "content": "[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：所有引用的逐项正确性检查（交叉验证）\n\n请对论文里**每一条引用**做严格核对。AI 写作最致命的问题之一就是 hallucinate 引用——作者错、年份错、venue 错、标题对不上，甚至整篇论文压根不存在。\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n检查范围\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n对 .bib 里的每一条 entry，以及正文里每一个 \\cite{} 出现的位置：\n\n□ 作者名拼写（中间名 / 缩写格式 / Unicode 字符）\n□ 年份（很多 AI 把 NeurIPS 2022 paper 写成 2021）\n□ 标题（前几个词最容易被 hallucinate 改写）\n□ Venue / 会议名（NeurIPS vs NIPS / EMNLP vs EMNLP Findings / arXiv vs published version）\n□ 卷期号 / 页码（journal 引用）\n□ DOI / arXiv ID（如果有）\n□ Paper 是否真实存在（hallucinated paper 是最严重的硬伤）\n□ 正文里的 cite 用得对吗（cite 了 paper A 但说的是 paper B 的结论）\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n执行方式：Main Agent 派 2-3 个 Worker 做交叉验证\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\nMain Agent 调用 spawn_worker 起 2-3 个独立 Worker，每个 Worker 独立完成全部引用的核对：\n\n- 每个 Worker 拿到完整的 .bib 文件 + 正文 \\cite 位置列表\n- 用 Semantic Scholar API / Google Scholar / arXiv 搜索每一条\n- 独立产出一份\"引用核对清单\"：每条标 ✓ 或 ✗ + 错误描述\n\nMain Agent 收到所有 Worker 的清单后做交叉比对：\n\n- ✅ 所有 Worker 都标 OK 的 → 接受\n- ⚠️ Worker 之间结论不一致的 → Main Agent 亲自再核一遍\n- ❌ 多数 Worker 标错的 → 必须修\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n修复策略\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n- 作者 / 年份 / 标题错 → 改成正确的\n- venue 错（如 arxiv 版本和会议版本搞混）→ 改成应该引用的版本\n- Paper 不存在（hallucinated）→ 删 cite + 重新找一个真实的同主题 paper 替代，如果找不到替代且这个 claim 离开 cite 就站不住，整段重写或删\n- cite 用错位置（A 引到了 B）→ 改成正确的 cite key\n\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n汇报内容\n══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n\n(a) 总共多少条引用？\n(b) 每个 Worker 各标出多少处问题？Worker 之间一致率？\n(c) Main Agent 交叉比对后，最终确认有问题的引用数量\n(d) 每一处的处理方式（修了 / 删了 / 替换了哪条新引用）\n(e) 修完后 .bib 重新生成 + LaTeX 编译通过 + bibtex 没 warning"
```

### User

_source line 418 · 2026-05-26T10:13:51.073Z_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：所有引用的逐项正确性检查（交叉验证）

请对论文里**每一条引用**做严格核对。AI 写作最致命的问题之一就是 hallucinate 引用——作者错、年份错、venue 错、标题对不上，甚至整篇论文压根不存在。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
检查范围
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

对 .bib 里的每一条 entry，以及正文里每一个 \cite{} 出现的位置：

□ 作者名拼写（中间名 / 缩写格式 / Unicode 字符）
□ 年份（很多 AI 把 NeurIPS 2022 paper 写成 2021）
□ 标题（前几个词最容易被 hallucinate 改写）
□ Venue / 会议名（NeurIPS vs NIPS / EMNLP vs EMNLP Findings / arXiv vs published version）
□ 卷期号 / 页码（journal 引用）
□ DOI / arXiv ID（如果有）
□ Paper 是否真实存在（hallucinated paper 是最严重的硬伤）
□ 正文里的 cite 用得对吗（cite 了 paper A 但说的是 paper B 的结论）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
执行方式：Main Agent 派 2-3 个 Worker 做交叉验证
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Main Agent 调用 spawn_worker 起 2-3 个独立 Worker，每个 Worker 独立完成全部引用的核对：

- 每个 Worker 拿到完整的 .bib 文件 + 正文 \cite 位置列表
- 用 Semantic Scholar API / Google Scholar / arXiv 搜索每一条
- 独立产出一份"引用核对清单"：每条标 ✓ 或 ✗ + 错误描述

Main Agent 收到所有 Worker 的清单后做交叉比对：

- ✅ 所有 Worker 都标 OK 的 → 接受
- ⚠️ Worker 之间结论不一致的 → Main Agent 亲自再核一遍
- ❌ 多数 Worker 标错的 → 必须修

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
修复策略
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

- 作者 / 年份 / 标题错 → 改成正确的
- venue 错（如 arxiv 版本和会议版本搞混）→ 改成应该引用的版本
- Paper 不存在（hallucinated）→ 删 cite + 重新找一个真实的同主题 paper 替代，如果找不到替代且这个 claim 离开 cite 就站不住，整段重写或删
- cite 用错位置（A 引到了 B）→ 改成正确的 cite key

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 总共多少条引用？
(b) 每个 Worker 各标出多少处问题？Worker 之间一致率？
(c) Main Agent 交叉比对后，最终确认有问题的引用数量
(d) 每一处的处理方式（修了 / 删了 / 替换了哪条新引用）
(e) 修完后 .bib 重新生成 + LaTeX 编译通过 + bibtex 没 warning

### Tool call

_source line 426 · 2026-05-26T10:14:24.762Z_

<details open><summary>🔧 Tool call — mcp__agent-ml-research__director_post_to_main_agent · `toolu_015xmhwqYxUHc26TsJU3tY7V`</summary>

```json
{
  "project": "schema_semantic_constrained_ie",
  "text": "**任务：全量引用逐项正确性审计（交叉验证）**\n\n论文目录：`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/_project/paper/`\n\n.bib 文件有多个，全部要覆盖（main.bib / relwork_new_entries.bib / new_citations.bib 等，先 ls 确认所有 .bib）。\n\n**执行方式：spawn 2-3 个 Worker 做独立交叉验证。**\n\n每个 Worker 拿到完整 .bib + 正文所有 \\cite{} 位置列表，对每一条 entry 独立核对：\n\n1. **作者名拼写**（中间名/缩写/Unicode）\n2. **年份**（常见错误：NeurIPS 2022 写成 2021）\n3. **标题**（前几个词最容易被 hallucinate 改写）\n4. **Venue/会议名**（NeurIPS vs NIPS / EMNLP vs EMNLP Findings / arXiv vs published version——如果有正式发表版本应引发表版）\n5. **卷期号/页码**（journal 引用）\n6. **DOI / arXiv ID**（如果有）\n7. **Paper 是否真实存在**（hallucinated paper 是最严重硬伤）\n8. **正文 cite 用对了吗**（cite 了 A 但说的是 B 的结论）\n\n每个 Worker 用 WebSearch / Semantic Scholar API / arXiv 搜索核实，产出完整核对清单（每条标 ✓ 或 ✗ + 错误描述）。\n\n**你收到所有 Worker 清单后做交叉比对：**\n- 全标 OK → 接受\n- Worker 之间不一致 → 你亲自再核一遍\n- 多数标错 → 必须修\n\n**修复策略：**\n- 作者/年份/标题错 → 改 .bib\n- venue 错（arXiv vs 已发表）→ 改成应引的版本\n- Paper 不存在（hallucinated）→ 删 cite + 找真实替代；找不到且 claim 离开 cite 站不住 → 整段重写或删\n- cite 用错位置 → 改正确的 cite key\n\n**修完后：**\n- 所有 .bib 更新\n- LaTeX 编译通过（`paper_compile_and_check`，paper_dir 用绝对路径）\n- bibtex 无 warning\n\n**汇报内容：**\n(a) 总共多少条引用\n(b) 每个 Worker 各标出多少处问题 + Worker 间一致率\n(c) 交叉比对后最终确认有问题的数量\n(d) 每处的处理方式（修/删/替换）\n(e) 编译通过确认\n\n优先级：高。今天是 ARR DDL。"
}
```
